# Plateau 13: context apparatus

Generated from `~/Downloads/protevi-response/work/context/` (do not edit by hand). Background sources for each variorum row, matched by Minnesota page. The green glosses in Column 4 explain words; this file points to where an argument is worked through elsewhere.

## ¶1 (Minnesota 424)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-425). Dumézil's magic sovereign as the model of capture: “He is the binding god, the knotting god, he is the god of the knot – he throws his net, he carries out his capture, and his emergence on the field of battle has the effect of freezing all the weapons.” “It concerns a civil capture, not at all a capture of war.” The question of the year is « what is this civil capture that is not made by means of arms? » *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 24-25.
- **Source** (ATP 424-425). Proposition X's despot who « binds » and its *nexum* (424-25, 428) are Dumézil's Varuna, the « binder »: “whoever respects satyam and sraddha (in other words, the various forms of correct behavior) is protected by Mitra, but whoever sins against them is immediately bound, in the most literal sense of the word” by Varuna. Of Uranos, likewise: “he seizes whomsoever he wishes, and he "binds" him. Once in his grasp, there is no possibility of resistance.” The Roman law of debt carries the same image: “Nexum is the state of the nexus, of the insolvent debtor who was, very literally, bound and subjugated by the creditor.” *Source:* `capture/dumezil_mitra_varuna.txt`, Mitra-Varuna, ch. VI, « Nexum and Mutuum » (PDF 95, 99).
- **Develops** (ATP 424-426). Dumézil's One-Eyed and One-Armed are read as the two halves of the State-work couple: “the one-eyed man, you understand, is the man of the sign”; “The one-handed man is the man of work.” Jünger's *Glass Bees* (quoted at 425-26) is summarized as “what is terrible about work is that a mutilation already precedes any accident”, which the plateau turns into « the mutilation is prior, preestablished » (447). *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 10-11.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.

## ¶7 (Minnesota 427)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Develops** (ATP 427-428). The theses on the origin of the State rejected at 427-28 are sorted from Engels into three kinds: exogenous (war), endogenous (private property, money), and « specific factors » (public functions; Engels, *Anti-Dühring*: “It is public functions that are at the origin of domination.”). All three are “these theses are, as one says in logic, tautologies, that is to say that they presuppose what is in question”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 16-17.
- **Develops** (ATP 427-428). The Asiatic mode of production, with its bibliography: Marx's *Grundrisse*, Wittfogel's *Oriental Despotism* (with Vidal-Naquet's preface), Tökei's *Sur le mode de production asiatique*, and the CERM collection. Marx's schema is “the subjunction of agricultural communes under a formal transcendent unity, the unity of the despot”. Why it embarrassed orthodox Marxism and was censored under Stalin: “It appeared that in the imperial formations, the imperial apparatus springs up fully formed!”, against the theory of stages. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 25-28.
- **Develops** (ATP 427-428). Code and overcoding, defined for the year: “The simple formula of the archaic empire is that the communitarian codes subsist, but they are overcoded.” “I therefore reserve the word ‘code’ for these interlacings of lineages and territories.” Overcoding is formalization by transcendence, as in the theory of types: “the formal unity cannot be of the same type as the set of formalized objects”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 28-29, 33-34.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.

On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Develops** (ATP 427-428). The theses on the origin of the State rejected at 427-28 are sorted from Engels into three kinds: exogenous (war), endogenous (private property, money), and « specific factors » (public functions; Engels, *Anti-Dühring*: “It is public functions that are at the origin of domination.”). All three are “these theses are, as one says in logic, tautologies, that is to say that they presuppose what is in question”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 16-17.
- **Develops** (ATP 427-428). The Asiatic mode of production, with its bibliography: Marx's *Grundrisse*, Wittfogel's *Oriental Despotism* (with Vidal-Naquet's preface), Tökei's *Sur le mode de production asiatique*, and the CERM collection. Marx's schema is “the subjunction of agricultural communes under a formal transcendent unity, the unity of the despot”. Why it embarrassed orthodox Marxism and was censored under Stalin: “It appeared that in the imperial formations, the imperial apparatus springs up fully formed!”, against the theory of stages. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 25-28.
- **Develops** (ATP 427-428). Code and overcoding, defined for the year: “The simple formula of the archaic empire is that the communitarian codes subsist, but they are overcoded.” “I therefore reserve the word ‘code’ for these interlacings of lineages and territories.” Overcoding is formalization by transcendence, as in the theory of types: “the formal unity cannot be of the same type as the set of formalized objects”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 28-29, 33-34.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.

## ¶9 (Minnesota 428)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Develops** (ATP 427-428). The theses on the origin of the State rejected at 427-28 are sorted from Engels into three kinds: exogenous (war), endogenous (private property, money), and « specific factors » (public functions; Engels, *Anti-Dühring*: “It is public functions that are at the origin of domination.”). All three are “these theses are, as one says in logic, tautologies, that is to say that they presuppose what is in question”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 16-17.
- **Develops** (ATP 427-428). The Asiatic mode of production, with its bibliography: Marx's *Grundrisse*, Wittfogel's *Oriental Despotism* (with Vidal-Naquet's preface), Tökei's *Sur le mode de production asiatique*, and the CERM collection. Marx's schema is “the subjunction of agricultural communes under a formal transcendent unity, the unity of the despot”. Why it embarrassed orthodox Marxism and was censored under Stalin: “It appeared that in the imperial formations, the imperial apparatus springs up fully formed!”, against the theory of stages. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 25-28.
- **Develops** (ATP 427-428). Code and overcoding, defined for the year: “The simple formula of the archaic empire is that the communitarian codes subsist, but they are overcoded.” “I therefore reserve the word ‘code’ for these interlacings of lineages and territories.” Overcoding is formalization by transcendence, as in the theory of types: “the formal unity cannot be of the same type as the set of formalized objects”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 28-29, 33-34.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-429). Mellaart's Çatal Hüyük and Jane Jacobs's *Economy of Cities* turn Childe's and Marx's schema around: an empire grafted directly onto hunter-gatherers, with wild seeds and small animals mixed « in a bag ». So “it is no longer the surplus that makes possible the stock, it is precisely the inverse”. “It is the town that invents agriculture.” The consequence for evolutionism: “we need a field of coexistence”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 44-47.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-429). Jacobs's « new obsidian » model restated with emphasis: “agriculture originates in the city and on the city’s land. It’s not born in the countryside, never, ever!” The capital sows the hybrids from the stock on its own land, and differential rent follows. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 139-140.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.

Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 429-431). The zigzag against linear evolution (429-31): Russian archaeologists observe that “nomadism must not be thought as a state of affairs preceding sedentarization”. The nomads of the steppes may be former cultivators who took up nomadizing. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 49.

## ¶14 (Minnesota 430)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 429-431). The zigzag against linear evolution (429-31): Russian archaeologists observe that “nomadism must not be thought as a state of affairs preceding sedentarization”. The nomads of the steppes may be former cultivators who took up nomadizing. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 49.

After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». - **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 429-431). The zigzag against linear evolution (429-31): Russian archaeologists observe that “nomadism must not be thought as a state of affairs preceding sedentarization”. The nomads of the steppes may be former cultivators who took up nomadizing. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 49.
- **Develops** (ATP 431-433). The convergent and divergent waves of Proposition XI come from physics (the « inverted wave »), set on the board. The convergent wave both wards off and anticipates; at its point of convergence it inverts. “This is the great mechanism, I think, of warding off: to prevent the resonance of centers of power.” Lizot's account of a Yanomami shaman's initiation, tracing lines between the spirits into a star around a mast, is the example of resonance kept apart from the social body. “the State apparatus is a threshold of consistency beyond so-called primitive groups, but ‘beyond’ does not mean ‘after’”. And “primitives never existed as they exist now. That is to say, they always only ever existed except by surviving”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 54-57.
- **Context** (ATP 431-433). Clastres on the State, in a late essay the plateau does not cite: “It is, in essence, a putting into play of centripetal force, which, when circumstances demand it, tends toward crushing the opposite centrifugal forces.” This is close to the convergent wave and the resonance of centers of power of 431-33. *Source:* `core/clastres_archeology_of_violence.txt`, Clastres, « Of Ethnocide », Archeology of Violence (PDF 109).

The convergent wave both wards off and anticipates; at its point of convergence it inverts. “This is the great mechanism, I think, of warding off: to prevent the resonance of centers of power.” Lizot's account of a Yanomami shaman's initiation, tracing lines between the spirits into a star around a mast, is the example of resonance kept apart from the social body. “the State apparatus is a threshold of consistency beyond so-called primitive groups, but ‘beyond’ does not mean ‘after’”. Clastres on the State, in a late essay the plateau does not cite: “It is, in essence, a putting into play of centripetal force, which, when circumstances demand it, tends toward crushing the opposite centrifugal forces.” This is close to the convergent wave and the resonance of centers of power of 431-33. *Source:* `core/clastres_archeology_of_violence.txt`, Clastres, « Of Ethnocide », Archeology of Violence (PDF 109).
- **Develops** (ATP 432-433). The two thresholds are coined in class: “The ‘town’ form, we could say – let us invent words, since we need to do that here – we will say that it is a threshold of transconsistency. The ‘State’ form, I would say that it is a threshold of intraconsistency.” “The town is a set of inputs and outputs.” The State instead makes the points it holds on a territory resonate. “It is a dynamic deterritorialization, which consists precisely in cutting oneself off from the backcountry. The ‘State’ form is a static deterritorialization”: the « planning of the territory » (Virilio). A « very bad metaphor » sets them side by side: towns as horizontal melodic lines, the State as vertical harmonic cuts. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 63-65.
- **Develops** (ATP 432-434). The town-State difference of 432-34, with the historians' material the plateau compresses. Braudel: ““Every time there are two runners: the State and the town. The State usually won.”” The Hanseatic League is a town form without a State: “They have no functionaries. They do not have any juridical personality. They don’t have an army.” After François Châtelet: “The magistrate would have been an invention of the town.” The State invented the functionary. Knossos against Mycenae (after Vernant), Babylon as « a network of cities » against Egypt, and the Orient as « rebel material within the urban system » (Braudel) round it out. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 58-62.
- **Develops** (ATP 432-435). The town-State difference is posed through capitalism: “how does it come about that the development of capitalism should have to pass through the ‘State’ pole?” One reason: “only States were able to literally afford the cost of appropriating the war machine”. Commercial towns need quick, cheap wars of mercenaries. “Carthage: it cannot support Hannibal.” States invent the war of materials, and then the military-industrial complex escapes them. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 15.

The convergent wave both wards off and anticipates; at its point of convergence it inverts. “This is the great mechanism, I think, of warding off: to prevent the resonance of centers of power.” Lizot's account of a Yanomami shaman's initiation, tracing lines between the spirits into a star around a mast, is the example of resonance kept apart from the social body. “the State apparatus is a threshold of consistency beyond so-called primitive groups, but ‘beyond’ does not mean ‘after’”. And “primitives never existed as they exist now. That is to say, they always only ever existed except by surviving”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 54-57.
- **Context** (ATP 431-433). Clastres on the State, in a late essay the plateau does not cite: “It is, in essence, a putting into play of centripetal force, which, when circumstances demand it, tends toward crushing the opposite centrifugal forces.” This is close to the convergent wave and the resonance of centers of power of 431-33. *Source:* `core/clastres_archeology_of_violence.txt`, Clastres, « Of Ethnocide », Archeology of Violence (PDF 109).
- **Develops** (ATP 432-433). The two thresholds are coined in class: “The ‘town’ form, we could say – let us invent words, since we need to do that here – we will say that it is a threshold of transconsistency. The ‘State’ form, I would say that it is a threshold of intraconsistency.” “The town is a set of inputs and outputs.” The State instead makes the points it holds on a territory resonate. “It is a dynamic deterritorialization, which consists precisely in cutting oneself off from the backcountry. The ‘State’ form is a static deterritorialization”: the « planning of the territory » (Virilio). A « very bad metaphor » sets them side by side: towns as horizontal melodic lines, the State as vertical harmonic cuts. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 63-65.
- **Develops** (ATP 432-434). The town-State difference of 432-34, with the historians' material the plateau compresses. Braudel: ““Every time there are two runners: the State and the town. The State usually won.”” The Hanseatic League is a town form without a State: “They have no functionaries. They do not have any juridical personality. They don’t have an army.” After François Châtelet: “The magistrate would have been an invention of the town.” The State invented the functionary. Knossos against Mycenae (after Vernant), Babylon as « a network of cities » against Egypt, and the Orient as « rebel material within the urban system » (Braudel) round it out. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 58-62.
- **Develops** (ATP 432-435). The town-State difference is posed through capitalism: “how does it come about that the development of capitalism should have to pass through the ‘State’ pole?” One reason: “only States were able to literally afford the cost of appropriating the war machine”. Commercial towns need quick, cheap wars of mercenaries. “Carthage: it cannot support Hannibal.” States invent the war of materials, and then the military-industrial complex escapes them. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 15.

## ¶20 (Minnesota 433)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 431-433). The convergent and divergent waves of Proposition XI come from physics (the « inverted wave »), set on the board. The convergent wave both wards off and anticipates; at its point of convergence it inverts. “This is the great mechanism, I think, of warding off: to prevent the resonance of centers of power.” Lizot's account of a Yanomami shaman's initiation, tracing lines between the spirits into a star around a mast, is the example of resonance kept apart from the social body. “the State apparatus is a threshold of consistency beyond so-called primitive groups, but ‘beyond’ does not mean ‘after’”. And “primitives never existed as they exist now. That is to say, they always only ever existed except by surviving”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 54-57.
- **Context** (ATP 431-433). Clastres on the State, in a late essay the plateau does not cite: “It is, in essence, a putting into play of centripetal force, which, when circumstances demand it, tends toward crushing the opposite centrifugal forces.” This is close to the convergent wave and the resonance of centers of power of 431-33. *Source:* `core/clastres_archeology_of_violence.txt`, Clastres, « Of Ethnocide », Archeology of Violence (PDF 109).
- **Develops** (ATP 432-433). They don’t have an army.” After François Châtelet: “The magistrate would have been an invention of the town.” The State invented the functionary. - **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 432-435). The town-State difference is posed through capitalism: “how does it come about that the development of capitalism should have to pass through the ‘State’ pole?” One reason: “only States were able to literally afford the cost of appropriating the war machine”. Commercial towns need quick, cheap wars of mercenaries. “Carthage: it cannot support Hannibal.” States invent the war of materials, and then the military-industrial complex escapes them. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 15.
- **Develops** (ATP 435-437). The four formations of 435-37 (anticipation–warding off, capture, polarization, the ecumenical) and the rule of their coexistence: “an oecumenic formation never proceeds via homogenization”; it straddles heterogeneous formations (commercial companies, metallurgists' guilds, Christianity, an art movement such as Dada and Surrealism). Against conspiracy talk about the Trilateral: “it is always a catastrophe when without precaution one projects one form onto another”. “If we want to understand the extent of our misfortune, we must realise that the enemy is multiple, there is not just one.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 66-69.
- **Develops** (ATP 435-437). Why social formations are defined by machinic processes rather than modes of production (435): “each process plays a preferential role in a certain type of formation, but it can very well be taken up in a completely different formation”. Towns ward off the State, and “You will also find anticipation-conjuration processes in capitalism to ward off the limits of capitalism.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 76.

- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.

Against conspiracy talk about the Trilateral: “it is always a catastrophe when without precaution one projects one form onto another”. “If we want to understand the extent of our misfortune, we must realise that the enemy is multiple, there is not just one.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 66-69.
- **Develops** (ATP 435-437). Why social formations are defined by machinic processes rather than modes of production (435): “each process plays a preferential role in a certain type of formation, but it can very well be taken up in a completely different formation”. Towns ward off the State, and “You will also find anticipation-conjuration processes in capitalism to ward off the limits of capitalism.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 76.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 437-438). The conditions for a model of primitive exchange (437-38): no stock, no money or market, no labor time, all three being acts of the State. “I cannot assume that primitive exchange implies a stockpile”. “At the most, I can say that it implies an elasticity of supply and demand.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 77-78.
- **Develops** (ATP 437-438). A marginalist textbook's sheep farmer deciding whether to hire one more shepherd « without making any change in his tools or in his constructions » gives the marginal object its sense: the last one before the assemblage must change. “This is the borderline shepherd, given the assemblage being considered.” From it: “there would be collective evaluations which would be of an anticipatory nature. What would they anticipate? They would anticipate the limit.” Kant's anticipations of perception are cited for the same structure. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 79-82.
- **Source** (ATP 437-438). The marginalism that 437-38 turns into a theory of the « last » object. Jevons's section heading reads “The final degree of utility and the Law of its Variation”. On water: “we usually have so much of it that its final degree of utility is reduced nearly to zero”. *Source:* `home-fields/jevons_1871.txt`, Jevons, The Theory of Political Economy (1871), ch. III.
- **Develops** (ATP 437-439). Marginalism and the « last glass » of 438 begin as an assignment: re-read the manuals. “It’s the theme of the last one, the last as determinant of the value of the object of exchange.” The café's last glass and the domestic scene's « last word » (Strindberg) each involve “an evaluation of the point at which one should go no further”. The section ends on oil: the banker Albin Chalandon on prices aligned to the cost of the last (offshore) barrel. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 70-72.

## ¶30 (Minnesota 437)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 435-437). The four formations of 435-37 (anticipation–warding off, capture, polarization, the ecumenical) and the rule of their coexistence: “an oecumenic formation never proceeds via homogenization”; it straddles heterogeneous formations (commercial companies, metallurgists' guilds, Christianity, an art movement such as Dada and Surrealism). Against conspiracy talk about the Trilateral: “it is always a catastrophe when without precaution one projects one form onto another”. “If we want to understand the extent of our misfortune, we must realise that the enemy is multiple, there is not just one.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 66-69.
- **Develops** (ATP 435-437). Why social formations are defined by machinic processes rather than modes of production (435): “each process plays a preferential role in a certain type of formation, but it can very well be taken up in a completely different formation”. Towns ward off the State, and “You will also find anticipation-conjuration processes in capitalism to ward off the limits of capitalism.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 76.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.

The conditions for a model of primitive exchange (437-38): no stock, no money or market, no labor time, all three being acts of the State. “I cannot assume that primitive exchange implies a stockpile”. “At the most, I can say that it implies an elasticity of supply and demand.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 77-78.

What would they anticipate? They would anticipate the limit.” Kant's anticipations of perception are cited for the same structure. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 79-82.
- **Source** (ATP 437-438). The marginalism that 437-38 turns into a theory of the « last » object. Jevons's section heading reads “The final degree of utility and the Law of its Variation”. On water: “we usually have so much of it that its final degree of utility is reduced nearly to zero”. *Source:* `home-fields/jevons_1871.txt`, Jevons, The Theory of Political Economy (1871), ch. III.
- **Develops** (ATP 437-439). Marginalism and the « last glass » of 438 begin as an assignment: re-read the manuals. “It’s the theme of the last one, the last as determinant of the value of the object of exchange.” The café's last glass and the domestic scene's « last word » (Strindberg) each involve “an evaluation of the point at which one should go no further”. The section ends on oil: the banker Albin Chalandon on prices aligned to the cost of the last (offshore) barrel. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 70-72.

Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.

Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 437-438). The conditions for a model of primitive exchange (437-38): no stock, no money or market, no labor time, all three being acts of the State. “I cannot assume that primitive exchange implies a stockpile”. “At the most, I can say that it implies an elasticity of supply and demand.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 77-78.
- **Develops** (ATP 437-438). A marginalist textbook's sheep farmer deciding whether to hire one more shepherd « without making any change in his tools or in his constructions » gives the marginal object its sense: the last one before the assemblage must change. “This is the borderline shepherd, given the assemblage being considered.” From it: “there would be collective evaluations which would be of an anticipatory nature. What would they anticipate? They would anticipate the limit.” Kant's anticipations of perception are cited for the same structure. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 79-82.
- **Source** (ATP 437-438). The marginalism that 437-38 turns into a theory of the « last » object. Jevons's section heading reads “The final degree of utility and the Law of its Variation”. On water: “we usually have so much of it that its final degree of utility is reduced nearly to zero”. *Source:* `home-fields/jevons_1871.txt`, Jevons, The Theory of Political Economy (1871), ch. III.
- **Develops** (ATP 437-439). Marginalism and the « last glass » of 438 begin as an assignment: re-read the manuals. “It’s the theme of the last one, the last as determinant of the value of the object of exchange.” The café's last glass and the domestic scene's « last word » (Strindberg) each involve “an evaluation of the point at which one should go no further”. The section ends on oil: the banker Albin Chalandon on prices aligned to the cost of the last (offshore) barrel. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 70-72.
- **Develops** (ATP 438-439). The two senses of « last » that 438-39 distinguish (penultimate and ultimate): “The marginal object is the penultimate or the limit. Beyond the marginal object, there is something else, the threshold once again.” Hence what primitive societies anticipate and ward off: “in the collective evaluation, what is anticipated is fundamentally the limit; what is warded off is fundamentally the threshold”. The café's « last glass » marks the break between two exercises of the same assemblage (“you have to stop drinking to be able to drink again”); beyond it lies another assemblage, alcoholism or the hospital; the domestic scene's last word, beyond which lies divorce. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 87-90.
- **Develops** (ATP 438-439). The abstract model of exchange behind 438-39: gatherers of wild seeds and makers of obsidian axes, with no shared language and no common measure of labor time, each evaluating its own marginal object. Hence “the objective relation in the exchange arises from two subjective series”. Past the marginal ax, the gatherers would have to clear land and become farmers: “So, they ward off the agricultural threshold by anticipating the “gathering” limit.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 91-92.

- **Develops** (ATP 438-439). The two senses of « last » that 438-39 distinguish (penultimate and ultimate): “The marginal object is the penultimate or the limit. Beyond the marginal object, there is something else, the threshold once again.” Hence what primitive societies anticipate and ward off: “in the collective evaluation, what is anticipated is fundamentally the limit; what is warded off is fundamentally the threshold”. The café's « last glass » marks the break between two exercises of the same assemblage (“you have to stop drinking to be able to drink again”); beyond it lies another assemblage, alcoholism or the hospital; the domestic scene's last word, beyond which lies divorce. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 87-90.
- **Develops** (ATP 438-439). The abstract model of exchange behind 438-39: gatherers of wild seeds and makers of obsidian axes, with no shared language and no common measure of labor time, each evaluating its own marginal object. Hence “the objective relation in the exchange arises from two subjective series”. Past the marginal ax, the gatherers would have to clear land and become farmers: “So, they ward off the agricultural threshold by anticipating the “gathering” limit.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 91-92.
- **Develops** (ATP 439). The evaluation of the last term comes at the start of the series, faster than the series itself. The marginalists' theory of trial and error: “They developed the notion of infinite speed of adjustment”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 87.

## ¶34 (Minnesota 439)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 437-439). Marginalism and the « last glass » of 438 begin as an assignment: re-read the manuals. “It’s the theme of the last one, the last as determinant of the value of the object of exchange.” The café's last glass and the domestic scene's « last word » (Strindberg) each involve “an evaluation of the point at which one should go no further”. The section ends on oil: the banker Albin Chalandon on prices aligned to the cost of the last (offshore) barrel. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 70-72.

Beyond the marginal object, there is something else, the threshold once again.” Hence what primitive societies anticipate and ward off: “in the collective evaluation, what is anticipated is fundamentally the limit; what is warded off is fundamentally the threshold”. The café's « last glass » marks the break between two exercises of the same assemblage (“you have to stop drinking to be able to drink again”); beyond it lies another assemblage, alcoholism or the hospital; the domestic scene's last word, beyond which lies divorce. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 87-90.

Hence “the objective relation in the exchange arises from two subjective series”. Past the marginal ax, the gatherers would have to clear land and become farmers: “So, they ward off the agricultural threshold by anticipating the “gathering” limit.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 91-92.

The marginalists' theory of trial and error: “They developed the notion of infinite speed of adjustment”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 4 (27 Nov 1979), PDF 87.

After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». - **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 440-441). The distinction behind 440-41 (territory and land, series and stock): “an assemblage consisting of a territory-code combination and an assemblage consisting of quite a different combination, land-overcoding”. Itinerance, not nomadism, follows a flow from territory to territory under codes. “this time around, there is an aggregate of territories exploited simultaneously, and this is what defines a land”. Proust's Albertine is the example of serial assemblages oriented to a limit; the revelation of the work is a threshold. “The limit is what separates one assemblage exercise from another assemblage exercise, the assemblage remaining of the same type. The threshold is quite different: it is the passage from one type of assemblage to another assemblage.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 99-101.

- **Develops** (ATP 441-442). Proposition XII's claim that surplus labor is not what exceeds labor but what constitutes it is announced as the hypothesis of the year: “it is surplus labour that constitutes labour. It is surplus labour that is constitutive of labour.” “There is no labour outside the State apparatus.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 6.
- **Develops** (ATP 441-444). The two dimensions of capture in each of its three segments (land, labor, money): a space of comparison and a point of monopolistic appropriation. Differential rent compares; absolute rent appropriates. “The space of comparison assumes the point of appropriation.” For money: “it is taxation that creates money”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 102-103.
- **Develops** (ATP 441-444). Pressed by Comtesse to name the despot's desire, Deleuze makes capture creative: “So, there is a creation of the land in distinction from territories. There is a creation of work in distinction from activity, from activities. There is a creation of money in distinction from trading” “desire is never, in any way whatsoever, behind the assemblage” “I have never defined the assemblage as an assemblage of power; I define it as an assemblage of desire.” Power « flows from it … secondarily ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 107-111 (exchange with Georges Comtesse).

The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 440-441). The distinction behind 440-41 (territory and land, series and stock): “an assemblage consisting of a territory-code combination and an assemblage consisting of quite a different combination, land-overcoding”. Itinerance, not nomadism, follows a flow from territory to territory under codes. “this time around, there is an aggregate of territories exploited simultaneously, and this is what defines a land”. Proust's Albertine is the example of serial assemblages oriented to a limit; the revelation of the work is a threshold. “The limit is what separates one assemblage exercise from another assemblage exercise, the assemblage remaining of the same type. The threshold is quite different: it is the passage from one type of assemblage to another assemblage.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 99-101.
- **Develops** (ATP 441-442). Proposition XII's claim that surplus labor is not what exceeds labor but what constitutes it is announced as the hypothesis of the year: “it is surplus labour that constitutes labour. It is surplus labour that is constitutive of labour.” “There is no labour outside the State apparatus.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 6.
- **Develops** (ATP 441-444). The two dimensions of capture in each of its three segments (land, labor, money): a space of comparison and a point of monopolistic appropriation. Differential rent compares; absolute rent appropriates. “The space of comparison assumes the point of appropriation.” For money: “it is taxation that creates money”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 102-103.
- **Develops** (ATP 441-444). Pressed by Comtesse to name the despot's desire, Deleuze makes capture creative: “So, there is a creation of the land in distinction from territories. There is a creation of work in distinction from activity, from activities. There is a creation of money in distinction from trading” “desire is never, in any way whatsoever, behind the assemblage” “I have never defined the assemblage as an assemblage of power; I define it as an assemblage of desire.” Power « flows from it … secondarily ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 107-111 (exchange with Georges Comtesse).

## ¶40 (Minnesota 442)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
