Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 441-442). Proposition XII's claim that surplus labor is not what exceeds labor but what constitutes it is announced as the hypothesis of the year: “it is surplus labour that constitutes labour. It is surplus labour that is constitutive of labour.” “There is no labour outside the State apparatus.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 6.
- **Develops** (ATP 441-444). The two dimensions of capture in each of its three segments (land, labor, money): a space of comparison and a point of monopolistic appropriation. Differential rent compares; absolute rent appropriates. “The space of comparison assumes the point of appropriation.” For money: “it is taxation that creates money”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 102-103.
- **Develops** (ATP 441-444). Pressed by Comtesse to name the despot's desire, Deleuze makes capture creative: “So, there is a creation of the land in distinction from territories. There is a creation of work in distinction from activity, from activities. There is a creation of money in distinction from trading” “desire is never, in any way whatsoever, behind the assemblage” “I have never defined the assemblage as an assemblage of power; I define it as an assemblage of desire.” Power « flows from it … secondarily ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 107-111 (exchange with Georges Comtesse).
- **Develops** (ATP 442-443). Money from tax rather than trade (442-43): commerce exists in primitive societies, but “it is the emperor who holds the monopoly of commerce”. Deleuze sides with the financial historians among “those who tend towards the idea of a sole origin, namely that it is always tax”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 18.

In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 441-444). The two dimensions of capture in each of its three segments (land, labor, money): a space of comparison and a point of monopolistic appropriation. Differential rent compares; absolute rent appropriates. “The space of comparison assumes the point of appropriation.” For money: “it is taxation that creates money”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 102-103.
- **Develops** (ATP 441-444). Pressed by Comtesse to name the despot's desire, Deleuze makes capture creative: “So, there is a creation of the land in distinction from territories. There is a creation of work in distinction from activity, from activities. There is a creation of money in distinction from trading” “desire is never, in any way whatsoever, behind the assemblage” “I have never defined the assemblage as an assemblage of power; I define it as an assemblage of desire.” Power « flows from it … secondarily ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 107-111 (exchange with Georges Comtesse).
- **Develops** (ATP 442-443). Money from tax rather than trade (442-43): commerce exists in primitive societies, but “it is the emperor who holds the monopoly of commerce”. Deleuze sides with the financial historians among “those who tend towards the idea of a sole origin, namely that it is always tax”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 18.
- **Develops** (ATP 443). Why indirect taxation shows that commerce presupposes taxation (443): direct tax is like rent, but “indirect tax enters into determining the price”. Éric Alliez's work on mercantilism is in the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 107.
- **Develops** (ATP 443-445). The three forms of capture (rent, profit, taxation) are first a « trinitary formula » of the archaic empire: “It is an apparatus of capture with three heads. And the three heads of the apparatus of capture, are: rent of the landlord, profit of the entrepreneur, tax of the despot.” The banker is the third functionary, and “the bank is not an intermediary”: it creates money, converting rent in kind into money rent. “It is not war that is the model of the State apparatus. It is much more like capture. It would be much more like hunting.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 18-20.
- **Develops** (ATP 443-453). A student presentation the plateau never cites. Éric Alliez (later the author of *Capital Times*) tests the tax-before-trade thesis on mercantilism: Hobbes's « venous » and « arterial » circuits of money in *Leviathan*, Colbert's general tax farm built on indirect taxes, and the Bank of England (1694) lending its own credit money to the State. His summary: “mercantilism is truly a political geometry of power in that all its work consists of an operation of axiomatization of production starting from the creation of a new space of appropriation and distribution which is the national territory”. Deleuze's verdict: “So, listen, there is nothing to add, since everything is confirmation.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 126-133 (presentation by Éric Alliez).

## ¶45 (Minnesota 443)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.

- **Develops** (ATP 444). The « black hole » of 444 joins the faciality plateau: the despotic face seen frontally, black hole on a white wall, is “the point of monopolistic appropriation that is displaced within a comparative space”. The evidence is Jacques Mercier's Ethiopian protective scrolls, eyes multiplying on parchment, and Byzantine frontality. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 104-105.

States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 443-445). The three forms of capture (rent, profit, taxation) are first a « trinitary formula » of the archaic empire: “It is an apparatus of capture with three heads. And the three heads of the apparatus of capture, are: rent of the landlord, profit of the entrepreneur, tax of the despot.” The banker is the third functionary, and “the bank is not an intermediary”: it creates money, converting rent in kind into money rent. “It is not war that is the model of the State apparatus. It is much more like capture. It would be much more like hunting.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 18-20.
- **Develops** (ATP 443-453). A student presentation the plateau never cites. Éric Alliez (later the author of *Capital Times*) tests the tax-before-trade thesis on mercantilism: Hobbes's « venous » and « arterial » circuits of money in *Leviathan*, Colbert's general tax farm built on indirect taxes, and the Bank of England (1694) lending its own credit money to the State. His summary: “mercantilism is truly a political geometry of power in that all its work consists of an operation of axiomatization of production starting from the creation of a new space of appropriation and distribution which is the national territory”. Deleuze's verdict: “So, listen, there is nothing to add, since everything is confirmation.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 126-133 (presentation by Éric Alliez).

## ¶49 (Minnesota 446)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 424-446). On Dumézil's « magic » capture (424-25) and the *nexum*: ““Magic” was a convenient and temporary word to distinguish this type of capture from other types of capture.” Its objective sense, which is that of 445-46 (capture as « already done », constituting what it captures): “The objective determination of magic is when, in fact, you define an operation on an aggregate, but at the same time, you cannot define the aggregate without having already placed the operation into it.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 112-113.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 443-453). A student presentation the plateau never cites. Éric Alliez (later the author of *Capital Times*) tests the tax-before-trade thesis on mercantilism: Hobbes's « venous » and « arterial » circuits of money in *Leviathan*, Colbert's general tax farm built on indirect taxes, and the Bank of England (1694) lending its own credit money to the State. His summary: “mercantilism is truly a political geometry of power in that all its work consists of an operation of axiomatization of production starting from the creation of a new space of appropriation and distribution which is the national territory”. Deleuze's verdict: “So, listen, there is nothing to add, since everything is confirmation.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 126-133 (presentation by Éric Alliez).

## ¶55 (Minnesota 448)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 443-453). A student presentation the plateau never cites. Éric Alliez (later the author of *Capital Times*) tests the tax-before-trade thesis on mercantilism: Hobbes's « venous » and « arterial » circuits of money in *Leviathan*, Colbert's general tax farm built on indirect taxes, and the Bank of England (1694) lending its own credit money to the State. His summary: “mercantilism is truly a political geometry of power in that all its work consists of an operation of axiomatization of production starting from the creation of a new space of appropriation and distribution which is the national territory”. Deleuze's verdict: “So, listen, there is nothing to add, since everything is confirmation.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 126-133 (presentation by Éric Alliez).
- **Develops** (ATP 448). The structural violence of 448 is worked through as two kinds of violence: “The State apparatus, for its part, has need of a police, well before having need of an army.” Of State violence: “Everything happens as if it was in the mode of the ‘already there’. It is always ‘already there’, it is always as already applied, it constantly presupposes itself.” “Whilst the violence of the war machine is a violence that is perpetually on the horizon.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 10.

- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 443-453). A student presentation the plateau never cites. Éric Alliez (later the author of *Capital Times*) tests the tax-before-trade thesis on mercantilism: Hobbes's « venous » and « arterial » circuits of money in *Leviathan*, Colbert's general tax farm built on indirect taxes, and the Bank of England (1694) lending its own credit money to the State. His summary: “mercantilism is truly a political geometry of power in that all its work consists of an operation of axiomatization of production starting from the creation of a new space of appropriation and distribution which is the national territory”. Deleuze's verdict: “So, listen, there is nothing to add, since everything is confirmation.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 126-133 (presentation by Éric Alliez).
- **Develops** (ATP 449). Why private property cannot grow out of the imperial functionaries (449): as Tökei says, “you can imagine that if they revolt against the Emperor they become little local despots, but you absolutely cannot imagine that they become private owners”. Everything in the archaic empire is public; the functionaries' revenue comes from the communes' tribute. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 33.
- **Develops** (ATP 449-451). Private property does not come from primitive communal property: “It is constituted starting from a system of public imperial property.” It appears later, by « a very specific social mechanism » (Proposition XIII). *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 17.
- **Develops** (ATP 449-451). Proposition XIII's freed slave and the « sphere of personal dependence » (449-51) are prepared through Tökei's essay on the birth of the Chinese elegy. “Who is the man of the sphere of personal dependency?” The man of the lament. In Tökei's answer, “it’s neither the outcast nor the imprisoned man; it’s the freed slave”, who becomes the Emperor's agent (his consilium, his fiscus). Caligula, Michelet's Louis XI, and Paul Veyne's Rome are the examples of this sphere, irreducible to public or private. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 5 (15 Jan 1980), PDF 95-98.
- **Develops** (ATP 449-451). Kafka's Great Wall and Castle mark the passage from the archaic « celestial bureaucracy » (Balazs) to the modern one: “neo-bureaucracy is not a pyramid-like bureaucracy at all; it's a bureaucracy of corridors, it's a bureaucracy of segments, a string of offices”. Orson Welles's vertical and longitudinal spaces are brought in as the cinema of the same passage. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 117-121.
- **Develops** (ATP 449-451). Who could want private property, when neither despot, functionary, nor commune can? Tökei's freed slave, “he is produced with the overcoding, but as uncoded”; with the Roman plebs (excluded from the *ager publicus*, granted private parcels), “it is at the level of the freed slave or the plebeian that the triple privatization takes place”: land, enterprise, commerce. Proposition XIII's thesis follows: “imperial overcoding necessarily entails – this is a possible hypothesis -- necessarily entails as a repercussion, as a correlate, some decoding of flows”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 122-125.
- **Develops** (ATP 449-451). The thesis of 449-51 (the State's overcoding produces the decoded flows that escape it), worked out head by head: “you will not overcode flows, you will not launch an overcoding apparatus without thereby causing decoded flows”. Public property brings forth private property (the freed slave), public works bring forth private slavery, and State metallic money brings forth bank money: “logically, what is called script currency is the expression of a relationship between two private persons”, convertible only through a central bank. The tie between them is “an alogical necessity, an alogical complementarity”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 134-142.

Tökei's freed slave, “he is produced with the overcoding, but as uncoded”; with the Roman plebs (excluded from the *ager publicus*, granted private parcels), “it is at the level of the freed slave or the plebeian that the triple privatization takes place”: land, enterprise, commerce. The tie between them is “an alogical necessity, an alogical complementarity”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 134-142.
- **Develops** (ATP 450-451). Childe's East-West dossier (450-51) as an « extrinsic complementarity »: the Aegean world was too poor to stock and too close not to trade and plunder (Crete and Mycenae are “These are Empires for laughs”). Metallurgists and merchants living off the Eastern stock: “the same figure who is overcoded in the East under the conditions of the archaic Empire, simultaneously exists as much less coded, as at the extreme decoded, in the Aegean world”. Balazs on Chinese emperors closing the mines explains why China, able to, did not invent capitalism. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 146-149.

And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 443-453). A student presentation the plateau never cites. Éric Alliez (later the author of *Capital Times*) tests the tax-before-trade thesis on mercantilism: Hobbes's « venous » and « arterial » circuits of money in *Leviathan*, Colbert's general tax farm built on indirect taxes, and the Bank of England (1694) lending its own credit money to the State. His summary: “mercantilism is truly a political geometry of power in that all its work consists of an operation of axiomatization of production starting from the creation of a new space of appropriation and distribution which is the national territory”. Deleuze's verdict: “So, listen, there is nothing to add, since everything is confirmation.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 126-133 (presentation by Éric Alliez).
- **Develops** (ATP 449-451). Private property does not come from primitive communal property: “It is constituted starting from a system of public imperial property.” It appears later, by « a very specific social mechanism » (Proposition XIII). *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 17.
- **Develops** (ATP 449-451). Proposition XIII's freed slave and the « sphere of personal dependence » (449-51) are prepared through Tökei's essay on the birth of the Chinese elegy. “Who is the man of the sphere of personal dependency?” The man of the lament. In Tökei's answer, “it’s neither the outcast nor the imprisoned man; it’s the freed slave”, who becomes the Emperor's agent (his consilium, his fiscus). Caligula, Michelet's Louis XI, and Paul Veyne's Rome are the examples of this sphere, irreducible to public or private. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 5 (15 Jan 1980), PDF 95-98.
- **Develops** (ATP 449-451). Kafka's Great Wall and Castle mark the passage from the archaic « celestial bureaucracy » (Balazs) to the modern one: “neo-bureaucracy is not a pyramid-like bureaucracy at all; it's a bureaucracy of corridors, it's a bureaucracy of segments, a string of offices”. Orson Welles's vertical and longitudinal spaces are brought in as the cinema of the same passage. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 117-121.
- **Develops** (ATP 449-451). Who could want private property, when neither despot, functionary, nor commune can? Tökei's freed slave, “he is produced with the overcoding, but as uncoded”; with the Roman plebs (excluded from the *ager publicus*, granted private parcels), “it is at the level of the freed slave or the plebeian that the triple privatization takes place”: land, enterprise, commerce. Proposition XIII's thesis follows: “imperial overcoding necessarily entails – this is a possible hypothesis -- necessarily entails as a repercussion, as a correlate, some decoding of flows”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 6 (22 Jan 1980), PDF 122-125.
- **Develops** (ATP 449-451). The thesis of 449-51 (the State's overcoding produces the decoded flows that escape it), worked out head by head: “you will not overcode flows, you will not launch an overcoding apparatus without thereby causing decoded flows”. Public property brings forth private property (the freed slave), public works bring forth private slavery, and State metallic money brings forth bank money: “logically, what is called script currency is the expression of a relationship between two private persons”, convertible only through a central bank. The tie between them is “an alogical necessity, an alogical complementarity”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 134-142.
- **Develops** (ATP 450-451). Childe's East-West dossier (450-51) as an « extrinsic complementarity »: the Aegean world was too poor to stock and too close not to trade and plunder (Crete and Mycenae are “These are Empires for laughs”). Metallurgists and merchants living off the Eastern stock: “the same figure who is overcoded in the East under the conditions of the archaic Empire, simultaneously exists as much less coded, as at the extreme decoded, in the Aegean world”. Balazs on Chinese emperors closing the mines explains why China, able to, did not invent capitalism. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 146-149.
- **Develops** (ATP 451-453). The second figure of the State (evolved empires, cities, feudalism) and its « topical conjunctions » (451-53): “relations of personal dependency are the expression of conjunctions, local, topical, qualified conjunctions between decoded flows”. The example the plateau does not give is courtly love. “Marriage is an overcoding, courtly love is a ... is something topical.” It is a relation of personal dependence of the knight on the lady, a conjunction of decoded flows of feeling. Feudal fief against Greek *kleros* (« false fiefs ») marks private property against function lands. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 150-153.

## ¶60 (Minnesota 451)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.

But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.

Éric Alliez (later the author of *Capital Times*) tests the tax-before-trade thesis on mercantilism: Hobbes's « venous » and « arterial » circuits of money in *Leviathan*, Colbert's general tax farm built on indirect taxes, and the Bank of England (1694) lending its own credit money to the State. His summary: “mercantilism is truly a political geometry of power in that all its work consists of an operation of axiomatization of production starting from the creation of a new space of appropriation and distribution which is the national territory”. Deleuze's verdict: “So, listen, there is nothing to add, since everything is confirmation.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 126-133 (presentation by Éric Alliez).

Private property does not come from primitive communal property: “It is constituted starting from a system of public imperial property.” It appears later, by « a very specific social mechanism » (Proposition XIII). *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 17.

- **Earlier version** (ATP 452-453). Marx on Adam Smith, eight years before session 8 of the 1979-80 seminar: “The deterritorialization of the capitalist form of wealth, whereby wealth is now only understood as a function of any-and-all productive activity and no longer as a function of some determinate element, neither the earth nor the state”. Political economy and psychoanalysis alike discover a deterritorialized essence, and “as soon as it gets revealed, it gets reterritorialized”, in private property or the family. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-9-April-18-72-English-Translation-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 18 Apr 1972.
- **Develops** (ATP 452-453). Proposition XIII's account of capitalism (452-53) starts from a logician's distinction between nominal and real definitions. The nominal definition comes from Marx's 1857 Introduction on Adam Smith: “Capital is the subjectivity of wealth.” Hence “capitalist property is the property of abstract rights convertible through all concrete determinations”; a capital whose formula is Kantian (“it is the relation between a subject posed as universal and an object posed as any object whatsoever”, the « object = X »). This is no longer a topical conjunction. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 155-162.
- **Develops** (ATP 452-453). The topical conjunction is set against “an axiomatic defined as a generalized conjugation of decoded flows”. The real definition, after Marx and Balibar's essay in *Reading Capital*: “the real definition of capital will be: the encounter between the two heterogeneous series, one of which results in the production of the naked worker and the other results in ... eh ... has as result the formation of the independent capitalist”. “There is a contingency, a fantastic contingency in the emergence of capitalism”: Rome had the plebs but not independent capital; Byzantium and twelfth-century China had elements of both, held back by imperial power. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 162-166.

## ¶63 (Minnesota 452)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 427-453). The two tempos of plateau 13, in the year of *Anti-Oedipus*: “despotic Asiatic social machines are of a truly synchronic nature - Marx's Asiatic state emerges in one blow”, while capitalism is « a diachronic machine » formed over centuries. And “it isn't capitalism that decodes the flows, rather, they are decoded on what we call the ruin and decline of great empires” (449-52). Capital is already “a machine for conjugating decoded flows” (452-53). The example is the Venetian School's Byzantine pyramids breaking apart around 1450. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-2-14-Dec-71-English-Update-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 14 Dec 1971, PDF 6-7.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 443-453). A student presentation the plateau never cites. Éric Alliez (later the author of *Capital Times*) tests the tax-before-trade thesis on mercantilism: Hobbes's « venous » and « arterial » circuits of money in *Leviathan*, Colbert's general tax farm built on indirect taxes, and the Bank of England (1694) lending its own credit money to the State. His summary: “mercantilism is truly a political geometry of power in that all its work consists of an operation of axiomatization of production starting from the creation of a new space of appropriation and distribution which is the national territory”. Deleuze's verdict: “So, listen, there is nothing to add, since everything is confirmation.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 126-133 (presentation by Éric Alliez).
- **Develops** (ATP 451-453). The second figure of the State (evolved empires, cities, feudalism) and its « topical conjunctions » (451-53): “relations of personal dependency are the expression of conjunctions, local, topical, qualified conjunctions between decoded flows”. The example the plateau does not give is courtly love. “Marriage is an overcoding, courtly love is a ... is something topical.” It is a relation of personal dependence of the knight on the lady, a conjunction of decoded flows of feeling. Feudal fief against Greek *kleros* (« false fiefs ») marks private property against function lands. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 7 (29 Jan 1980), PDF 150-153.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 452-453). Marx on Adam Smith, eight years before session 8 of the 1979-80 seminar: “The deterritorialization of the capitalist form of wealth, whereby wealth is now only understood as a function of any-and-all productive activity and no longer as a function of some determinate element, neither the earth nor the state”. Political economy and psychoanalysis alike discover a deterritorialized essence, and “as soon as it gets revealed, it gets reterritorialized”, in private property or the family. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-9-April-18-72-English-Translation-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 18 Apr 1972.
- **Develops** (ATP 452-453). Proposition XIII's account of capitalism (452-53) starts from a logician's distinction between nominal and real definitions. The nominal definition comes from Marx's 1857 Introduction on Adam Smith: “Capital is the subjectivity of wealth.” Hence “capitalist property is the property of abstract rights convertible through all concrete determinations”; a capital whose formula is Kantian (“it is the relation between a subject posed as universal and an object posed as any object whatsoever”, the « object = X »). This is no longer a topical conjunction. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 155-162.
- **Develops** (ATP 452-453). The topical conjunction is set against “an axiomatic defined as a generalized conjugation of decoded flows”. The real definition, after Marx and Balibar's essay in *Reading Capital*: “the real definition of capital will be: the encounter between the two heterogeneous series, one of which results in the production of the naked worker and the other results in ... eh ... has as result the formation of the independent capitalist”. “There is a contingency, a fantastic contingency in the emergence of capitalism”: Rome had the plebs but not independent capital; Byzantium and twelfth-century China had elements of both, held back by imperial power. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 162-166.

- **Develops** (ATP 452-453). Proposition XIII's account of capitalism (452-53) starts from a logician's distinction between nominal and real definitions. The nominal definition comes from Marx's 1857 Introduction on Adam Smith: “Capital is the subjectivity of wealth.” Hence “capitalist property is the property of abstract rights convertible through all concrete determinations”; a capital whose formula is Kantian (“it is the relation between a subject posed as universal and an object posed as any object whatsoever”, the « object = X »). This is no longer a topical conjunction. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 155-162.
- **Develops** (ATP 452-453). The topical conjunction is set against “an axiomatic defined as a generalized conjugation of decoded flows”. The real definition, after Marx and Balibar's essay in *Reading Capital*: “the real definition of capital will be: the encounter between the two heterogeneous series, one of which results in the production of the naked worker and the other results in ... eh ... has as result the formation of the independent capitalist”. “There is a contingency, a fantastic contingency in the emergence of capitalism”: Rome had the plebs but not independent capital; Byzantium and twelfth-century China had elements of both, held back by imperial power. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 162-166.
- **Develops** (ATP 453). On capitalism and ground rent (453): “a fundamental tendency of ... of capitalism is the inhibition or even the liquidation of land rent”. Three historical solutions: the English alliance of the bourgeoisie with rent-free American wheat (the moving frontier pays no rent), the French alliance with the small peasant, and then Algeria, where expropriated vineyards paid no rent. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 158-160.
- **Develops** (ATP 453-455). Why capital needs States it does not need in principle (453-55). Jean Saint-Geours (*Pouvoir et finance*), a banker, on Eurodollars and petrodollars: “he calls it "la stateless currency”, “stateless currency””. The example the plateau lacks: Eisenhower cutting NASA's moon funding while the Soviets built « a belt around the earth ». “The state will make the necessary reterritorializations to prevent capital flows from being decoded too quickly or too radically.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 169-170.

The nominal definition comes from Marx's 1857 Introduction on Adam Smith: “Capital is the subjectivity of wealth.” Hence “capitalist property is the property of abstract rights convertible through all concrete determinations”; a capital whose formula is Kantian (“it is the relation between a subject posed as universal and an object posed as any object whatsoever”, the « object = X »). This is no longer a topical conjunction. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 155-162.
- **Develops** (ATP 452-453). The topical conjunction is set against “an axiomatic defined as a generalized conjugation of decoded flows”. The real definition, after Marx and Balibar's essay in *Reading Capital*: “the real definition of capital will be: the encounter between the two heterogeneous series, one of which results in the production of the naked worker and the other results in ... eh ... has as result the formation of the independent capitalist”. “There is a contingency, a fantastic contingency in the emergence of capitalism”: Rome had the plebs but not independent capital; Byzantium and twelfth-century China had elements of both, held back by imperial power. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 162-166.
- **Develops** (ATP 453). On capitalism and ground rent (453): “a fundamental tendency of ... of capitalism is the inhibition or even the liquidation of land rent”. Three historical solutions: the English alliance of the bourgeoisie with rent-free American wheat (the moving frontier pays no rent), the French alliance with the small peasant, and then Algeria, where expropriated vineyards paid no rent. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 158-160.
- **Develops** (ATP 453-455). Why capital needs States it does not need in principle (453-55). Jean Saint-Geours (*Pouvoir et finance*), a banker, on Eurodollars and petrodollars: “he calls it "la stateless currency”, “stateless currency””. The example the plateau lacks: Eisenhower cutting NASA's moon funding while the Soviets built « a belt around the earth ». “The state will make the necessary reterritorializations to prevent capital flows from being decoded too quickly or too radically.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 169-170.

## ¶67 (Minnesota 454)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 453-455). Why capital needs States it does not need in principle (453-55). Jean Saint-Geours (*Pouvoir et finance*), a banker, on Eurodollars and petrodollars: “he calls it "la stateless currency”, “stateless currency””. The example the plateau lacks: Eisenhower cutting NASA's moon funding while the Soviets built « a belt around the earth ». “The state will make the necessary reterritorializations to prevent capital flows from being decoded too quickly or too radically.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 169-170.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 454-455). The code-axiomatic distinction of 454-55, as « five differences ». “Capitalism doesn’t restore code; it ushers in an axiomatic of decoded flows, which only happens to restore code.” A code is heterogeneous, « made up of shreds », with secret zones around collectively invested organs; “We ought to maintain a categorical distinction between secrecy and objective concealment.” “Just as code is the system of finite debt and finite economics, so too is axiomatics the system of infinite debt.” Scientific axiomatics are the expression of a social axiomatic, not its model. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-8-March-7-72-English-Translation-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 7 Mar 1972, PDF 1-6.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 454-455). On psychoanalysis's money, a one-line version of why the axiomatic needs no transcendent model: “Codes need belief—absolutely not so with axiomatics; who cares?”. *Source:* `purdue/Anti-Oedipus-I/AO-I-6-February-15-72-English-Translation-2023.txt`, seminar Anti-Oedipus I, 15 Feb 1972.
- **Develops** (ATP 454-455). The axiomatic of capital (454-55, 460) is first set against imperial overcoding: “Axiomatics is immanent to its models. This is why it is not a formalization.” Modern social formations working « in a certain axiomatic mode » cannot be assimilated to the archaic empires: “Axiomatics does not operate through overcoding, it operates through something else.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34.
- **Develops** (ATP 454-455). What an axiomatic does that nothing else does (454): it is “the only discourse that allows a direct comparison, a direct confrontation, a direct comparison between heterogeneous sets or domains insofar as being heterogeneous”, without homogenizing them. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 180-182.
- **Develops** (ATP 454-455). Formalization against axiomatization, Russell against Hilbert, with the theory of types (Russell's « typical Frenchman » and the liar). “a formalization, here’s what it is: it is the emission (dégagement) and the determination of formal relations between elements specified according to such or such a type”. The archaic State overcoding the communes is a formalization; so “unlike the archaic State, the modern State has ceased to be a model to be realized; it has become a model of realization in relation to an axiomatic”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 183-186.
- **Develops** (ATP 454-456). States as models of realization (454-56): “the State apparatuses are the models of realization of this axiomatic”. Heterogeneous models of one axiomatic, so very different States. The archaic State was a model in another sense: “It is no longer the model in the sense of "transcendent model"; it is, on the contrary, a model in the immanent sense of model of realization in relation to an axiomatic”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 171-172.

States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 454-456). States as models of realization (454-56): “the State apparatuses are the models of realization of this axiomatic”. Heterogeneous models of one axiomatic, so very different States. The archaic State was a model in another sense: “It is no longer the model in the sense of "transcendent model"; it is, on the contrary, a model in the immanent sense of model of realization in relation to an axiomatic”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 171-172.
- **Develops** (ATP 456). The nation as the State's form under capital (456): “The nation State is how to create a land and a people.” It is made « with blood … with lashes from whips … with music » (Verdi in Italy, not Wagner in Germany). A people is a decoded population. “The nation has been defined by crushing nationalitarian phenomena.” The term is Guattari's. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 171.

Guattari's own working of the distinction of 456-58, written in the same years: “It is by means of machinic enslavement that it settles in the heart of individuals.” The integration of workers' elites rests on their involvement with « their profession, their technology, their machines », not only on interest. “Machinic enslavement does not coincide with social subjugation.” *Source:* `cerfi/felix-guattari-soft-subversions.txt`, Soft Subversions, Guattari's essay on capital as the integral of power formations, § 4 (PDF 260-262).

Subjection, by grammar: “one is subjected to the machine. It is not the machine that subjects us, when we are subjected.” Bradbury's wall television marks the passage between them: “The television has become the wall, and at the same time, the listener, the user of the television, has become a constituent part of the programme itself.” Ergonomics' « man-machine » and « men-machines » systems are set against each other (adaptation against selection and communication). Three ages of the machine follow: archaic enslavement, subjection (which “subjection can obviously only come about with the separation out of private persons”, perfected under the wage), and the new enslavement of cybernetic machines. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 35-40.

## ¶72 (Minnesota 456)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 428-457). Mumford's megamachine is taken literally, by the nineteenth-century definition of a machine (solid elements with specialized functions transmitting movement under human control): the archaic empire is “it is a megamachine whose elements are men”. “It is the great archaic despotic empires that invent machinic enslavement. But they don’t have technical machines at all.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 34-35, 39.

States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Three ages of the machine follow: archaic enslavement, subjection (which “subjection can obviously only come about with the separation out of private persons”, perfected under the wage), and the new enslavement of cybernetic machines. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 35-40.
- **Develops** (ATP 457-458). The economic side of the distinction, as « a law … added to Marxism »: “the greater the predominance of variable capital in relation to constant capital, the more social subjection”; “The more constant capital [writing on the board] takes and tends to take relative importance in relation to variable capital, the more machinic enslavement there is.” Automation, subcontracting, and the black market, analyzed first by the Italian autonomists, belong here. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 2 (13 Nov 1979), PDF 41-42.

## ¶78 (Minnesota 459)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.

## ¶80 (Minnesota 460)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 460-473). Proposition XIV's claim that the axiomatic of capital is not a metaphor (461) is set as the task: “Capitalism is truly an axiomatic, given what mathematicians call an axiomatic, and it is more than a metaphor.” After Blanché and Bourbaki, three group axioms are realized in two heterogeneous models (the addition of real numbers, the composition of displacements in Euclidean space), and the definition follows: “we call “axiomatic” a set of functional relations between unspecified elements which are embodied or which are realized in formal relations and qualified or specified elements, specific to each of these fields of realization”. Four problems are announced, which become Proposition XIV's sections: isomorphy and heteromorphy of models, saturation, the power of the nondenumerable, and undecidable propositions. “Does every axiomatic generate and secrete its undecidable propositions?” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 172-178.

## ¶82 (Minnesota 461)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 460-473). Proposition XIV's claim that the axiomatic of capital is not a metaphor (461) is set as the task: “Capitalism is truly an axiomatic, given what mathematicians call an axiomatic, and it is more than a metaphor.” After Blanché and Bourbaki, three group axioms are realized in two heterogeneous models (the addition of real numbers, the composition of displacements in Euclidean space), and the definition follows: “we call “axiomatic” a set of functional relations between unspecified elements which are embodied or which are realized in formal relations and qualified or specified elements, specific to each of these fields of realization”. Four problems are announced, which become Proposition XIV's sections: isomorphy and heteromorphy of models, saturation, the power of the nondenumerable, and undecidable propositions. “Does every axiomatic generate and secrete its undecidable propositions?” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 172-178.
- **Develops** (ATP 461). Against the reading of the axiomatic as an automaton (461: the axiomatic as experimentation): “it is rather the constitution of an entirely new mode of experimentation”. Hence, for the political comparison to come, “one cannot form from the axiomatic the idea of ... of a kind of constitution of infallible automatic knowledge”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 189-190.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 461-473). The political stakes of Proposition XIV, three years earlier: “is an organization possible which is not modelled on the apparatus of the State, even to prefigure the State to come? Perhaps a war-machine with its lines of flight?” Against the « supremely cunning Master »: “The most centralized State is not at all the master of its plans”; « it is also an experimenter ». On minorities (469-71): “all kinds of minority questions - linguistic, ethnic, regional, about sex, or youth - resurge not only as archaisms, but in up-to-date revolutionary forms”. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 145-47 (PDF 160-62).
- **Develops** (ATP 461-473). A third concept beside topical conjunction and generalized conjugation, the one Proposition XIV ends on (connections against conjugations, 472-73): “there might still be something else, and that's the connections of flows”, « creative » or « anticipatory », proceeding by a calculus of problems (Bouligand's « problem element » against the « global synthesis »). “There are plenty of things that escape through the mesh of an axiomatic”: the world of problem-events. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 198-200.

- **Develops** (ATP 462). Virilio's formula, used at 462: “The totalitarian State is not at all the State at its maximum; it is the minimum State.” The example: “to let prices vary freely, you need a State apparatus, that is the minimum State”. The capitalist demand for less State is not the anarchist « no State at all ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 167-168.
- **Develops** (ATP 462-463). Section 1 of Proposition XIV (« Addition, subtraction », 461-63), with the cases the plateau names in a line. The first bipolarity: “a tendency to add axioms, to always add axioms to the axiomatic of capital, and a contrary tendency to withdraw axioms, to operate with a minimum of axioms”. The totalitarian pole: “a totalitarian State is a State which retains, at the level of axioms, only the axioms necessary for participation in the foreign market”, liquidating the internal market (Chile and the Chicago school, whose official says “the rest is only a consequence”; Giscard's France tending the same way). The social-democratic pole multiplies axioms (full employment). “Do you want an axiom for women? Okay, we'll make axioms for women.” Brazil in 1979-80, with exiles returning before the coming elections, is « suspense » between the two. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 11 (11 Mar 1980), PDF 243-247.
- **Develops** (ATP 462-463). The distinction of 462-63: “there is no question of ... confusing totalitarianism and fascism”. Franco's Spain was totalitarian, not fascist. “what defines the fascist State is much more this: it is the conversion of industry into an arms industry”, the internal production of capital (Schacht) for an expansionism the totalitarian State does not have. Its internal axioms proliferate, with “the production of ersatz (substitute) products, which you do not find at all in totalitarian regimes”. It is a « monster kind of composition » that both subtracts and adds axioms. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 11 (11 Mar 1980), PDF 247-248.
