- **Develops** (ATP 460-473). Proposition XIV's claim that the axiomatic of capital is not a metaphor (461) is set as the task: “Capitalism is truly an axiomatic, given what mathematicians call an axiomatic, and it is more than a metaphor.” After Blanché and Bourbaki, three group axioms are realized in two heterogeneous models (the addition of real numbers, the composition of displacements in Euclidean space), and the definition follows: “we call “axiomatic” a set of functional relations between unspecified elements which are embodied or which are realized in formal relations and qualified or specified elements, specific to each of these fields of realization”. Four problems are announced, which become Proposition XIV's sections: isomorphy and heteromorphy of models, saturation, the power of the nondenumerable, and undecidable propositions. “Does every axiomatic generate and secrete its undecidable propositions?” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 172-178.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 461-473). The political stakes of Proposition XIV, three years earlier: “is an organization possible which is not modelled on the apparatus of the State, even to prefigure the State to come? Perhaps a war-machine with its lines of flight?” Against the « supremely cunning Master »: “The most centralized State is not at all the master of its plans”; « it is also an experimenter ». On minorities (469-71): “all kinds of minority questions - linguistic, ethnic, regional, about sex, or youth - resurge not only as archaisms, but in up-to-date revolutionary forms”. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 145-47 (PDF 160-62).
- **Develops** (ATP 461-473). A third concept beside topical conjunction and generalized conjugation, the one Proposition XIV ends on (connections against conjugations, 472-73): “there might still be something else, and that's the connections of flows”, « creative » or « anticipatory », proceeding by a calculus of problems (Bouligand's « problem element » against the « global synthesis »). “There are plenty of things that escape through the mesh of an axiomatic”: the world of problem-events. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 198-200.
- **Develops** (ATP 462). Virilio's formula, used at 462: “The totalitarian State is not at all the State at its maximum; it is the minimum State.” The example: “to let prices vary freely, you need a State apparatus, that is the minimum State”. The capitalist demand for less State is not the anarchist « no State at all ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 167-168.
- **Develops** (ATP 462-463). Section 1 of Proposition XIV (« Addition, subtraction », 461-63), with the cases the plateau names in a line. The first bipolarity: “a tendency to add axioms, to always add axioms to the axiomatic of capital, and a contrary tendency to withdraw axioms, to operate with a minimum of axioms”. The totalitarian pole: “a totalitarian State is a State which retains, at the level of axioms, only the axioms necessary for participation in the foreign market”, liquidating the internal market (Chile and the Chicago school, whose official says “the rest is only a consequence”; Giscard's France tending the same way). The social-democratic pole multiplies axioms (full employment). “Do you want an axiom for women? Okay, we'll make axioms for women.” Brazil in 1979-80, with exiles returning before the coming elections, is « suspense » between the two. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 11 (11 Mar 1980), PDF 243-247.
- **Develops** (ATP 462-463). The distinction of 462-63: “there is no question of ... confusing totalitarianism and fascism”. Franco's Spain was totalitarian, not fascist. “what defines the fascist State is much more this: it is the conversion of industry into an arms industry”, the internal production of capital (Schacht) for an expansionism the totalitarian State does not have. Its internal axioms proliferate, with “the production of ersatz (substitute) products, which you do not find at all in totalitarian regimes”. It is a « monster kind of composition » that both subtracts and adds axioms. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 11 (11 Mar 1980), PDF 247-248.

## ¶86 (Minnesota 463)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 460-473). Proposition XIV's claim that the axiomatic of capital is not a metaphor (461) is set as the task: “Capitalism is truly an axiomatic, given what mathematicians call an axiomatic, and it is more than a metaphor.” After Blanché and Bourbaki, three group axioms are realized in two heterogeneous models (the addition of real numbers, the composition of displacements in Euclidean space), and the definition follows: “we call “axiomatic” a set of functional relations between unspecified elements which are embodied or which are realized in formal relations and qualified or specified elements, specific to each of these fields of realization”. Four problems are announced, which become Proposition XIV's sections: isomorphy and heteromorphy of models, saturation, the power of the nondenumerable, and undecidable propositions. “Does every axiomatic generate and secrete its undecidable propositions?” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 172-178.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 461-473). The political stakes of Proposition XIV, three years earlier: “is an organization possible which is not modelled on the apparatus of the State, even to prefigure the State to come? Perhaps a war-machine with its lines of flight?” Against the « supremely cunning Master »: “The most centralized State is not at all the master of its plans”; « it is also an experimenter ». On minorities (469-71): “all kinds of minority questions - linguistic, ethnic, regional, about sex, or youth - resurge not only as archaisms, but in up-to-date revolutionary forms”. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 145-47 (PDF 160-62).
- **Develops** (ATP 461-473). A third concept beside topical conjunction and generalized conjugation, the one Proposition XIV ends on (connections against conjugations, 472-73): “there might still be something else, and that's the connections of flows”, « creative » or « anticipatory », proceeding by a calculus of problems (Bouligand's « problem element » against the « global synthesis »). “There are plenty of things that escape through the mesh of an axiomatic”: the world of problem-events. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 198-200.
- **Develops** (ATP 462-463). Section 1 of Proposition XIV (« Addition, subtraction », 461-63), with the cases the plateau names in a line. The first bipolarity: “a tendency to add axioms, to always add axioms to the axiomatic of capital, and a contrary tendency to withdraw axioms, to operate with a minimum of axioms”. The totalitarian pole: “a totalitarian State is a State which retains, at the level of axioms, only the axioms necessary for participation in the foreign market”, liquidating the internal market (Chile and the Chicago school, whose official says “the rest is only a consequence”; Giscard's France tending the same way). The social-democratic pole multiplies axioms (full employment). “Do you want an axiom for women? Okay, we'll make axioms for women.” Brazil in 1979-80, with exiles returning before the coming elections, is « suspense » between the two. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 11 (11 Mar 1980), PDF 243-247.
- **Develops** (ATP 462-463). The distinction of 462-63: “there is no question of ... confusing totalitarianism and fascism”. Franco's Spain was totalitarian, not fascist. “what defines the fascist State is much more this: it is the conversion of industry into an arms industry”, the internal production of capital (Schacht) for an expansionism the totalitarian State does not have. Its internal axioms proliferate, with “the production of ersatz (substitute) products, which you do not find at all in totalitarian regimes”. It is a « monster kind of composition » that both subtracts and adds axioms. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 11 (11 Mar 1980), PDF 247-248.
- **Develops** (ATP 463). Section 2 (« Saturation », 463) through the chapter of *Capital* III on the tendency of the rate of profit to fall: “one speaks of saturation when one is in a state where adding an axion is no longer possible without the whole axiomatic becoming contradictory”. Constant against variable capital is Marx's own discovery, and Deleuze complains that the Pléiade edition omits the passage where Marx says so. Against capitalism's talk of external limits (energy, food): “these limits to capitalism are immanent limits of capitalism itself”. The session breaks off on a disturbance outside, and the next week opens with an attack by a « commando ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 11 (11 Mar 1980), PDF 248-252.
- **Develops** (ATP 463). Saturation (463) as an immanent limit, like the series one plus a half plus a quarter: “an immanent limit is such that, in fact, as it is produced and is not encountered from the outside by the system, it is internal: the more the system approaches this limit, the more it pushes back this limit”. Capital takes over Clausewitz's pair: “The aim of capital is to produce for capital; that’s its aim. What is the objective of the capital? The objective is unlimited. Capital’s objective is to produce in order to produce.” The contradiction is resolved by “periodic depreciation ... "periodic depreciation of existing capital and creation of new capital."”, with the oil-nuclear switch as the current case. The consequence is an « irreversible arrow »: capitalism cannot go back even to a state it was in a few years before. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 255-258.
- **Develops** (ATP 463-471). The example of the immanent limit, from Robert Linhart's *Le sucre et la faim* (1980) on northeast Brazil: “hunger is not encountered as the result of scarcity; it is produced as the result of an organization”. Sugar monoculture wipes out the peasants' plots. Then cane alcohol for cars needs special engines (Volkswagen) and foreign capital, which makes the arrow irreversible: « “This is the hunger" -- f-a-i-m -- "of the northeast that will allow cars to roll throughout the entire country.”” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 258-263.

The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 460-473). Proposition XIV's claim that the axiomatic of capital is not a metaphor (461) is set as the task: “Capitalism is truly an axiomatic, given what mathematicians call an axiomatic, and it is more than a metaphor.” After Blanché and Bourbaki, three group axioms are realized in two heterogeneous models (the addition of real numbers, the composition of displacements in Euclidean space), and the definition follows: “we call “axiomatic” a set of functional relations between unspecified elements which are embodied or which are realized in formal relations and qualified or specified elements, specific to each of these fields of realization”. Four problems are announced, which become Proposition XIV's sections: isomorphy and heteromorphy of models, saturation, the power of the nondenumerable, and undecidable propositions. “Does every axiomatic generate and secrete its undecidable propositions?” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 172-178.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 461-473). The political stakes of Proposition XIV, three years earlier: “is an organization possible which is not modelled on the apparatus of the State, even to prefigure the State to come? Perhaps a war-machine with its lines of flight?” Against the « supremely cunning Master »: “The most centralized State is not at all the master of its plans”; « it is also an experimenter ». On minorities (469-71): “all kinds of minority questions - linguistic, ethnic, regional, about sex, or youth - resurge not only as archaisms, but in up-to-date revolutionary forms”. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 145-47 (PDF 160-62).
- **Develops** (ATP 461-473). A third concept beside topical conjunction and generalized conjugation, the one Proposition XIV ends on (connections against conjugations, 472-73): “there might still be something else, and that's the connections of flows”, « creative » or « anticipatory », proceeding by a calculus of problems (Bouligand's « problem element » against the « global synthesis »). “There are plenty of things that escape through the mesh of an axiomatic”: the world of problem-events. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 198-200.
- **Develops** (ATP 463-471). The example of the immanent limit, from Robert Linhart's *Le sucre et la faim* (1980) on northeast Brazil: “hunger is not encountered as the result of scarcity; it is produced as the result of an organization”. Sugar monoculture wipes out the peasants' plots. Then cane alcohol for cars needs special engines (Volkswagen) and foreign capital, which makes the arrow irreversible: « “This is the hunger" -- f-a-i-m -- "of the northeast that will allow cars to roll throughout the entire country.”” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 258-263.
- **Develops** (ATP 464-466). Section 3 of Proposition XIV (« Models, isomorphy », 464-66): “States can be heterogeneous and yet be isomorphic”. The ground is that “There is only one and the same global market”, the socialist countries included. Three bipolarities: totalitarian against social-democratic; West-East, imposed after 1918; and North-South, organized by the center itself. « North-South » is fictitious geography: “It is a vector: the "North-South" vector.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 266-269.

## ¶90 (Minnesota 465)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 436-465). States as « models of realization » for the single world market of capital, isomorphic but heterogeneous (436, 464-65): “this isomorphy does not at all imply homogeneity”. Samir Amin's thesis that “there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic” grounds the point: international relations bring different modes of production into play. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 3 (20 Nov 1979), PDF 67-68.
- **Develops** (ATP 460-473). Proposition XIV's claim that the axiomatic of capital is not a metaphor (461) is set as the task: “Capitalism is truly an axiomatic, given what mathematicians call an axiomatic, and it is more than a metaphor.” After Blanché and Bourbaki, three group axioms are realized in two heterogeneous models (the addition of real numbers, the composition of displacements in Euclidean space), and the definition follows: “we call “axiomatic” a set of functional relations between unspecified elements which are embodied or which are realized in formal relations and qualified or specified elements, specific to each of these fields of realization”. Four problems are announced, which become Proposition XIV's sections: isomorphy and heteromorphy of models, saturation, the power of the nondenumerable, and undecidable propositions. “Does every axiomatic generate and secrete its undecidable propositions?” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 172-178.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 461-473). The political stakes of Proposition XIV, three years earlier: “is an organization possible which is not modelled on the apparatus of the State, even to prefigure the State to come? Perhaps a war-machine with its lines of flight?” Against the « supremely cunning Master »: “The most centralized State is not at all the master of its plans”; « it is also an experimenter ». On minorities (469-71): “all kinds of minority questions - linguistic, ethnic, regional, about sex, or youth - resurge not only as archaisms, but in up-to-date revolutionary forms”. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 145-47 (PDF 160-62).
- **Develops** (ATP 461-473). A third concept beside topical conjunction and generalized conjugation, the one Proposition XIV ends on (connections against conjugations, 472-73): “there might still be something else, and that's the connections of flows”, « creative » or « anticipatory », proceeding by a calculus of problems (Bouligand's « problem element » against the « global synthesis »). “There are plenty of things that escape through the mesh of an axiomatic”: the world of problem-events. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 198-200.
- **Develops** (ATP 463-471). The example of the immanent limit, from Robert Linhart's *Le sucre et la faim* (1980) on northeast Brazil: “hunger is not encountered as the result of scarcity; it is produced as the result of an organization”. Sugar monoculture wipes out the peasants' plots. Then cane alcohol for cars needs special engines (Volkswagen) and foreign capital, which makes the arrow irreversible: « “This is the hunger" -- f-a-i-m -- "of the northeast that will allow cars to roll throughout the entire country.”” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 258-263.
- **Develops** (ATP 464-466). Section 3 of Proposition XIV (« Models, isomorphy », 464-66): “States can be heterogeneous and yet be isomorphic”. The ground is that “There is only one and the same global market”, the socialist countries included. Three bipolarities: totalitarian against social-democratic; West-East, imposed after 1918; and North-South, organized by the center itself. « North-South » is fictitious geography: “It is a vector: the "North-South" vector.” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 266-269.
- **Source** (ATP 465-468). The periphery's axioms of 465-68, as the seminar (session 12) takes them from Amin: “the peripheral formations all tend to converge upon a typical model, characterized by the dominance of agrarian capital and ancillary (comprador) commercial capital”. *Source:* `braudel-amin/amin_unequal.txt`, Amin, Unequal Development (1976), ch. 4 (PDF 195).
- **Develops** (ATP 465-469). Mode and relation of production, defined « at my own risk »: “a mode of production is closely determined by the role of the force of labor”; the relation is the status of wealth titles. Capitalist States are capitalist in both. The socialist bureaucracies: “a country is socialist-bureaucratic when the wealth security, or the element which enables the manufacture of capital, is not capital, but is the [economic] plan”. Most Third World States are capitalist in relation but not in mode, and after Samir Amin's *Unequal Development* these are not survivals: “it’s a question here of peripheral forms”, as slavery in the American South was. The periphery's axioms are large landholding, « comprador trade » (a Maoist category, with Linhart's peasants), and proletarianization as marginalization. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 269-274.

Most Third World States are capitalist in relation but not in mode, and after Samir Amin's *Unequal Development* these are not survivals: “it’s a question here of peripheral forms”, as slavery in the American South was. The periphery's axioms are large landholding, « comprador trade » (a Maoist category, with Linhart's peasants), and proletarianization as marginalization. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 269-274.

## ¶94 (Minnesota 467)
- **Develops** (ATP 421-467). The war machine of 421-22 and 467, after Virilio: “the war machine does not even need to have wars as its object, since it discovers its object in a peace of terror”. The Cold War names it. Wars become parts of the peace; the enemy becomes « any enemy whatsoever », seen as a cloud on a radar screen or from a nuclear submarine. Nuclear war makes “all the points of space exist in a topological neighborhood”, an immanent limit like saturation. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 293-294.
- **Develops** (ATP 422-467). The « unspecified enemy » of 422 and 467 arrives through a crisis at Vincennes: the University Council, citing drug dealing, had called in security guards to check ID cards and opened the campus to the narcotics squad, ahead of the forced move to Saint-Denis. Deleuze reads it as the militarization of civilian functions. In Germany and Italy (terrorism) and in France (Mesrine), “the police demand, in more or less direct forms, for… the right to shoot on sight”, and with it “the enemy becomes any enemy whatsoever (l’ennemi quelconque)”. “any enemy whatsoever is typically an axiomatic notion, that's obvious; it's part of the theory of any object whatsoever”. After Virilio, “the organization of security based on the management of small insecurities”: emergency-medicine cars crossing the city, the city as « permanent stress ». The session is otherwise given over to organizing (meeting in groups at the gate, moving the class to the Cartoucherie) to protect the auditors, « about half » the room. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 10 (4 Mar 1980), PDF 207-209.
- **Develops** (ATP 460-473). Proposition XIV's claim that the axiomatic of capital is not a metaphor (461) is set as the task: “Capitalism is truly an axiomatic, given what mathematicians call an axiomatic, and it is more than a metaphor.” After Blanché and Bourbaki, three group axioms are realized in two heterogeneous models (the addition of real numbers, the composition of displacements in Euclidean space), and the definition follows: “we call “axiomatic” a set of functional relations between unspecified elements which are embodied or which are realized in formal relations and qualified or specified elements, specific to each of these fields of realization”. Four problems are announced, which become Proposition XIV's sections: isomorphy and heteromorphy of models, saturation, the power of the nondenumerable, and undecidable propositions. “Does every axiomatic generate and secrete its undecidable propositions?” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 172-178.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 461-473). The political stakes of Proposition XIV, three years earlier: “is an organization possible which is not modelled on the apparatus of the State, even to prefigure the State to come? Perhaps a war-machine with its lines of flight?” Against the « supremely cunning Master »: “The most centralized State is not at all the master of its plans”; « it is also an experimenter ». On minorities (469-71): “all kinds of minority questions - linguistic, ethnic, regional, about sex, or youth - resurge not only as archaisms, but in up-to-date revolutionary forms”. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 145-47 (PDF 160-62).
- **Develops** (ATP 461-473). A third concept beside topical conjunction and generalized conjugation, the one Proposition XIV ends on (connections against conjugations, 472-73): “there might still be something else, and that's the connections of flows”, « creative » or « anticipatory », proceeding by a calculus of problems (Bouligand's « problem element » against the « global synthesis »). “There are plenty of things that escape through the mesh of an axiomatic”: the world of problem-events. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 198-200.
- **Develops** (ATP 463-471). The example of the immanent limit, from Robert Linhart's *Le sucre et la faim* (1980) on northeast Brazil: “hunger is not encountered as the result of scarcity; it is produced as the result of an organization”. Sugar monoculture wipes out the peasants' plots. Then cane alcohol for cars needs special engines (Volkswagen) and foreign capital, which makes the arrow irreversible: « “This is the hunger" -- f-a-i-m -- "of the northeast that will allow cars to roll throughout the entire country.”” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 258-263.
- **Source** (ATP 465-468). The periphery's axioms of 465-68, as the seminar (session 12) takes them from Amin: “the peripheral formations all tend to converge upon a typical model, characterized by the dominance of agrarian capital and ancillary (comprador) commercial capital”. *Source:* `braudel-amin/amin_unequal.txt`, Amin, Unequal Development (1976), ch. 4 (PDF 195).
- **Develops** (ATP 465-469). Mode and relation of production, defined « at my own risk »: “a mode of production is closely determined by the role of the force of labor”; the relation is the status of wealth titles. Capitalist States are capitalist in both. The socialist bureaucracies: “a country is socialist-bureaucratic when the wealth security, or the element which enables the manufacture of capital, is not capital, but is the [economic] plan”. Most Third World States are capitalist in relation but not in mode, and after Samir Amin's *Unequal Development* these are not survivals: “it’s a question here of peripheral forms”, as slavery in the American South was. The periphery's axioms are large landholding, « comprador trade » (a Maoist category, with Linhart's peasants), and proletarianization as marginalization. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 269-274.

- **Develops** (ATP 469). The credit to Negri at 469 comes with the political moment: the autonomists « are all in prison, or almost all of them », and Deleuze names “the considerable contribution made by the Italian autonomists, starting with Toni Negri, to have pushed very far with a Marxist analysis (but of a new kind) of labour in the contemporary State”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 6.
- **Develops** (ATP 469). Precarious, temporary, and undeclared work in center and periphery alike (469), read from the law of composition “as constant capital becomes more and more important in relation to variable capital”, with the students « at the forefront » of precarity. Credit goes to Negri and the autonomists, and to Karl Heinz Roth, *L'autre mouvement ouvrier*. And “the form of erratic work has a force… it has very, very great revolutionary potential”: struggles move from the factory to the neighborhood. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 264-266.
- **Develops** (ATP 469). The forecast behind 469's precarious work: “we feel that we are heading towards an abolition of wage labor”, with capital remaining the relation of production while the mode becomes « neo-slavery » (pittance wages, company stores). The great accomplishment of recent capitalism was “the integration of the European proletariat into the enterprise of global exploitation” (Baran and Sweezy on American workers and Vietnam). A revolutionary connection would be an alliance of the exploited Third World with workers at the center no longer held by the wage: « students and Third World ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 299-301.
- **Develops** (ATP 469-471). The seminar ends on Proposition XIV's minorities (469-71), with three notions where the plateau has two: “I would say of a majority that it is a set which is adequate for its own criterion”, the « ordinary man » (Yann Moulier on the unions' criterion: skilled worker, white, over thirty-five); the minority as subset, which social democracy can grant axioms; and “the minoritarian movement, as opposed to the minority; it is not an aggregate, it is a becoming”. Becoming is double and dissymmetrical, from the majoritarian and the minoritarian sides at once, as in Faulkner's *Intruder in the Dust*: “either we become black, or we end up as fascists”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 302-305.

## ¶100 (Minnesota 469)
- **Develops** (ATP 460-473). Proposition XIV's claim that the axiomatic of capital is not a metaphor (461) is set as the task: “Capitalism is truly an axiomatic, given what mathematicians call an axiomatic, and it is more than a metaphor.” After Blanché and Bourbaki, three group axioms are realized in two heterogeneous models (the addition of real numbers, the composition of displacements in Euclidean space), and the definition follows: “we call “axiomatic” a set of functional relations between unspecified elements which are embodied or which are realized in formal relations and qualified or specified elements, specific to each of these fields of realization”. Four problems are announced, which become Proposition XIV's sections: isomorphy and heteromorphy of models, saturation, the power of the nondenumerable, and undecidable propositions. “Does every axiomatic generate and secrete its undecidable propositions?” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 172-178.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 461-473). The political stakes of Proposition XIV, three years earlier: “is an organization possible which is not modelled on the apparatus of the State, even to prefigure the State to come? Perhaps a war-machine with its lines of flight?” Against the « supremely cunning Master »: “The most centralized State is not at all the master of its plans”; « it is also an experimenter ». On minorities (469-71): “all kinds of minority questions - linguistic, ethnic, regional, about sex, or youth - resurge not only as archaisms, but in up-to-date revolutionary forms”. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 145-47 (PDF 160-62).
- **Develops** (ATP 461-473). A third concept beside topical conjunction and generalized conjugation, the one Proposition XIV ends on (connections against conjugations, 472-73): “there might still be something else, and that's the connections of flows”, « creative » or « anticipatory », proceeding by a calculus of problems (Bouligand's « problem element » against the « global synthesis »). “There are plenty of things that escape through the mesh of an axiomatic”: the world of problem-events. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 198-200.
- **Develops** (ATP 463-471). The example of the immanent limit, from Robert Linhart's *Le sucre et la faim* (1980) on northeast Brazil: “hunger is not encountered as the result of scarcity; it is produced as the result of an organization”. Sugar monoculture wipes out the peasants' plots. Then cane alcohol for cars needs special engines (Volkswagen) and foreign capital, which makes the arrow irreversible: « “This is the hunger" -- f-a-i-m -- "of the northeast that will allow cars to roll throughout the entire country.”” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 258-263.
- **Develops** (ATP 465-469). Mode and relation of production, defined « at my own risk »: “a mode of production is closely determined by the role of the force of labor”; the relation is the status of wealth titles. Capitalist States are capitalist in both. The socialist bureaucracies: “a country is socialist-bureaucratic when the wealth security, or the element which enables the manufacture of capital, is not capital, but is the [economic] plan”. Most Third World States are capitalist in relation but not in mode, and after Samir Amin's *Unequal Development* these are not survivals: “it’s a question here of peripheral forms”, as slavery in the American South was. The periphery's axioms are large landholding, « comprador trade » (a Maoist category, with Linhart's peasants), and proletarianization as marginalization. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 269-274.
- **Develops** (ATP 468-469). The internal Third Worlds of 468-69: “the center is more and more deterritorialized, and moreover, the centers are more and more deterritorialized”, so the center can be realized in the periphery (multinationals, decisions taken in Brazil) and the periphery hollowed out within the center. “The Fourth Worlds are the Third Worlds which are constituted in the very center” The axiomatic sustains not only heterogeneity but « a real polymorphy ». *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 275-277.
- **Develops** (ATP 469). The credit to Negri at 469 comes with the political moment: the autonomists « are all in prison, or almost all of them », and Deleuze names “the considerable contribution made by the Italian autonomists, starting with Toni Negri, to have pushed very far with a Marxist analysis (but of a new kind) of labour in the contemporary State”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 1 (6 Nov 1979), PDF 6.

And “the form of erratic work has a force… it has very, very great revolutionary potential”: struggles move from the factory to the neighborhood. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 264-266.
- **Develops** (ATP 469). The forecast behind 469's precarious work: “we feel that we are heading towards an abolition of wage labor”, with capital remaining the relation of production while the mode becomes « neo-slavery » (pittance wages, company stores). The great accomplishment of recent capitalism was “the integration of the European proletariat into the enterprise of global exploitation” (Baran and Sweezy on American workers and Vietnam). - **Develops** (ATP 469-471). The seminar ends on Proposition XIV's minorities (469-71), with three notions where the plateau has two: “I would say of a majority that it is a set which is adequate for its own criterion”, the « ordinary man » (Yann Moulier on the unions' criterion: skilled worker, white, over thirty-five); the minority as subset, which social democracy can grant axioms; and “the minoritarian movement, as opposed to the minority; it is not an aggregate, it is a becoming”. Becoming is double and dissymmetrical, from the majoritarian and the minoritarian sides at once, as in Faulkner's *Intruder in the Dust*: “either we become black, or we end up as fascists”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 302-305.

## ¶101 (Minnesota 470)
- **Develops** (ATP 460-473). Proposition XIV's claim that the axiomatic of capital is not a metaphor (461) is set as the task: “Capitalism is truly an axiomatic, given what mathematicians call an axiomatic, and it is more than a metaphor.” After Blanché and Bourbaki, three group axioms are realized in two heterogeneous models (the addition of real numbers, the composition of displacements in Euclidean space), and the definition follows: “we call “axiomatic” a set of functional relations between unspecified elements which are embodied or which are realized in formal relations and qualified or specified elements, specific to each of these fields of realization”. Four problems are announced, which become Proposition XIV's sections: isomorphy and heteromorphy of models, saturation, the power of the nondenumerable, and undecidable propositions. “Does every axiomatic generate and secrete its undecidable propositions?” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 172-178.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 461-473). The political stakes of Proposition XIV, three years earlier: “is an organization possible which is not modelled on the apparatus of the State, even to prefigure the State to come? Perhaps a war-machine with its lines of flight?” Against the « supremely cunning Master »: “The most centralized State is not at all the master of its plans”; « it is also an experimenter ». On minorities (469-71): “all kinds of minority questions - linguistic, ethnic, regional, about sex, or youth - resurge not only as archaisms, but in up-to-date revolutionary forms”. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 145-47 (PDF 160-62).
- **Develops** (ATP 461-473). A third concept beside topical conjunction and generalized conjugation, the one Proposition XIV ends on (connections against conjugations, 472-73): “there might still be something else, and that's the connections of flows”, « creative » or « anticipatory », proceeding by a calculus of problems (Bouligand's « problem element » against the « global synthesis »). “There are plenty of things that escape through the mesh of an axiomatic”: the world of problem-events. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 198-200.
- **Develops** (ATP 463-471). The example of the immanent limit, from Robert Linhart's *Le sucre et la faim* (1980) on northeast Brazil: “hunger is not encountered as the result of scarcity; it is produced as the result of an organization”. Sugar monoculture wipes out the peasants' plots. Then cane alcohol for cars needs special engines (Volkswagen) and foreign capital, which makes the arrow irreversible: « “This is the hunger" -- f-a-i-m -- "of the northeast that will allow cars to roll throughout the entire country.”” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 12 (18 Mar 1980), PDF 258-263.
- **Develops** (ATP 469-471). The seminar ends on Proposition XIV's minorities (469-71), with three notions where the plateau has two: “I would say of a majority that it is a set which is adequate for its own criterion”, the « ordinary man » (Yann Moulier on the unions' criterion: skilled worker, white, over thirty-five); the minority as subset, which social democracy can grant axioms; and “the minoritarian movement, as opposed to the minority; it is not an aggregate, it is a becoming”. Becoming is double and dissymmetrical, from the majoritarian and the minoritarian sides at once, as in Faulkner's *Intruder in the Dust*: “either we become black, or we end up as fascists”. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 302-305.
- **Develops** (ATP 470-473). Section 5 (« Undecidable propositions », 471-73): “the capitalist axiomatic, or capitalism as axiomatic, does not cease generating undecidable propositions”. An undecidable is « an included third ». Capital tries two ways to recover them, adding axioms and extermination, and neither works: “the camps multiply the people locked up there rather than suppress them”, as organized hunger multiplies the hungry. The four undecidable flows, from McNamara's World Bank speeches: “flows of raw materials, flows of food materials, flows of population, flows of urbanization-slums”. The method: “a system or a social field is not defined by its contradictions”, but by its lines of flight. Connections, not conjugations, are the revolutionary possibility. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 295-299.

The method: “a system or a social field is not defined by its contradictions”, but by its lines of flight. Connections, not conjugations, are the revolutionary possibility. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 295-299.

## ¶106 (Minnesota 472)
- **Develops** (ATP 460-473). Proposition XIV's claim that the axiomatic of capital is not a metaphor (461) is set as the task: “Capitalism is truly an axiomatic, given what mathematicians call an axiomatic, and it is more than a metaphor.” After Blanché and Bourbaki, three group axioms are realized in two heterogeneous models (the addition of real numbers, the composition of displacements in Euclidean space), and the definition follows: “we call “axiomatic” a set of functional relations between unspecified elements which are embodied or which are realized in formal relations and qualified or specified elements, specific to each of these fields of realization”. Four problems are announced, which become Proposition XIV's sections: isomorphy and heteromorphy of models, saturation, the power of the nondenumerable, and undecidable propositions. “Does every axiomatic generate and secrete its undecidable propositions?” *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 8 (5 Feb 1980), PDF 172-178.
- **Earlier version** (ATP 461-473). The political stakes of Proposition XIV, three years earlier: “is an organization possible which is not modelled on the apparatus of the State, even to prefigure the State to come? Perhaps a war-machine with its lines of flight?” Against the « supremely cunning Master »: “The most centralized State is not at all the master of its plans”; « it is also an experimenter ». On minorities (469-71): “all kinds of minority questions - linguistic, ethnic, regional, about sex, or youth - resurge not only as archaisms, but in up-to-date revolutionary forms”. *Source:* `flight/dialogues.txt`, Dialogues, « Many Politics », pp. 145-47 (PDF 160-62).
- **Develops** (ATP 461-473). A third concept beside topical conjunction and generalized conjugation, the one Proposition XIV ends on (connections against conjugations, 472-73): “there might still be something else, and that's the connections of flows”, « creative » or « anticipatory », proceeding by a calculus of problems (Bouligand's « problem element » against the « global synthesis »). “There are plenty of things that escape through the mesh of an axiomatic”: the world of problem-events. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 9 (26 Feb 1980), PDF 198-200.
- **Develops** (ATP 470-473). Section 5 (« Undecidable propositions », 471-73): “the capitalist axiomatic, or capitalism as axiomatic, does not cease generating undecidable propositions”. An undecidable is « an included third ». Capital tries two ways to recover them, adding axioms and extermination, and neither works: “the camps multiply the people locked up there rather than suppress them”, as organized hunger multiplies the hungry. The four undecidable flows, from McNamara's World Bank speeches: “flows of raw materials, flows of food materials, flows of population, flows of urbanization-slums”. The method: “a system or a social field is not defined by its contradictions”, but by its lines of flight. Connections, not conjugations, are the revolutionary possibility. *Source:* `purdue/ATP-V-79-80-Complete-English.txt`, seminar 1979-80, session 13 (25 Mar 1980), PDF 295-299.
